It just feels like... like my life is already over. So... yeah. Just most of the time.\n\nDoctor: How has your sleep been since this started?\n\nPatient: Not great. I mostly just... lie awake for hours. Just listening. Checking the door. And then... when I finally do sleep, I wake up way before dawn. Usually shaking. And I just... I can't get back under. So I don't sleep much.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually tired or lacking energy?\n\nPatient: Yeah... I'm tired all the time. I don't really do much... I might just stay in bed for half the day. But even then... if I just have to take a shower or... or reply to a text from someone... it feels like I just worked a whole shift or something. It's just... too much.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating or making decisions since this started?\n\nPatient: I don't know. I guess... maybe? I just... I don't really do much that takes concentration anyway. So... I'm not sure.\n\nDoctor: Since this began, has there been any period lasting two months or more when you felt mostly back to your usual self?\n\nPatient: No... I don't think so. I haven't really felt like myself in... I don't know how long. It's just been like this.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts about killing yourself or ending your life?\n\nPatient: I... yeah. I just... I keep thinking I don't want to wake up anymore. It's just... I guess my friend found me the other day, and... she said I was just saying out loud that I couldn't keep living like this. I don't know.\n\nDoctor: Have you thought about a specific way you might kill yourself?\n\nPatient: No... not really. I don't... I haven't figured anything out like that. I just... mostly I just want it to be over.\n\nDoctor: Since this began, have you noticed any change in your appetite?\n\nPatient: Yeah... I guess. I just go most of the day without eating. It's... I don't know, food just tastes like cardboard anyway. My jeans have gotten pretty loose... I have to knot the waistband now.\n\nDoctor: Have other people noticed that you are moving or speaking noticeably more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: I don't really know what other people notice. I guess... sometimes I just pace. Like, from the kitchen to the window, over and over until my legs ache. But then... other days, I just can't. Everything is just... slow. Even just trying to make coffee feels like I'm lifting wet cement. I don't know.\n\nDoctor: In the past two weeks, have your depression symptoms been noticeably worse than they were during most of the preceding two years?\n\nPatient: I don't know. It's just... always bad. I haven't really kept track of time like that. It just feels the same. Heavy.\n\nDoctor: How much have these symptoms interfered with your ability to manage your daily responsibilities, such as work, school, or basic self-care?\n\nPatient: I guess... a lot. I haven't been taking any of my freelance art stuff. I just... can't. I don't go to community events anymore, or play soccer. My friend... she has to come over a lot. Just to make sure I'm okay. Like... making sure I eat something, or shower. Sometimes she just stays because... I don't know, it's probably better I'm not by myself right now.\n\nDoctor: Have there been any stretches of at least two months during these past two years when you had no significant depression or loss of interest?\n\nPatient: No. Not really. I don't think there's ever been a time like that. It's just... always there.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
